The following description:

The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register which controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection. This register holds the physical page number (PPN) of the root page table (its supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB), an address space identifier (ASID) which facilitates address-translation fences on a per-addressspace basis, and the MODE field, which selects the current address-translation scheme.

Figure 63. Supervisor address translation and protection ( satp ) register when SXLEN=32.
| 31 | 30 | 22 21 |
|-------------|-------------|------------|
| MODE (WARL) | ASID (WARL) | PPN (WARL) |
| 1 | 9 | 22 |

Figure 64. Supervisor address translation and protection ( satp ) register when SXLEN=64, for MODE values Bare, Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
| 63 | 60 59 | 44 43 |
|-------------|-------------|------------|
| MODE (WARL) | ASID (WARL) | PPN (WARL) |
| 4 | 16 | 44 |

Regarding the fields and behavior of satp:
* Storing a PPN in satp, rather than a physical address, supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB for RV32.
* The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers.
* When MODE=Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses, and there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme described in Section 3.7.
* To select MODE=Bare, software must write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 30-0 when SXLEN=32, or bits 59-0 when SXLEN=64).
* Attempting to select MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining fields has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
* When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3 are designated for custom use.
* When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7] is not equal to 3 are reserved for future standard use.
* When SXLEN=64, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.
* When SXLEN=32, the only other valid setting for MODE is Sv32.
* When SXLEN=64, three paged virtual-memory schemes are defined: Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
* When SXLEN=64, the remaining MODE settings are reserved for future use and may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.
* If satp is written with an unsupported MODE, the entire write has no effect; no fields in satp are modified.
* The number of implemented ASID bits (ASIDLEN) may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the ASID field, then reading back the value in satp to see which bit positions in the ASID field hold a one.
* If ASIDLEN > 0, ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] is writable.
* The maximal value of ASIDLEN (ASIDMAX) is 9 for Sv32 or 16 for Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
* The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.
* Executions of the address-translation algorithm may only begin using a given value of satp when satp is active.
* Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.
* Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations, nor does it imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.

Table 40. Encoding of satp MODE field.
| SXLEN=32 Value | SXLEN=32 Name | SXLEN=32 Description |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 1 | Sv32 | Page-based 32-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.3). |

| SXLEN=64 Value | SXLEN=64 Name | SXLEN=64 Description |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 1-7 | - | Reserved for standard use |
| 8 | Sv39 | Page-based 39-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.4). |
| 9 | Sv48 | Page-based 48-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.5). |
| 10 | Sv57 | Page-based 57-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.6). |
| 11 | Sv64 | Reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing. |
| 12-13 | - | Reserved for standard use |
| 14-15 | - | Designated for custom use |
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register which controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection.
RULE 2: The satp register holds the physical page number (PPN) of the root page table, which is its supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB.
RULE 3: The satp register holds an address space identifier (ASID) which facilitates address-translation fences on a per-addressspace basis.
RULE 4: The satp register contains a MODE field which selects the current address-translation scheme.
RULE 5: When SXLEN=32, the MODE field of satp is at bit 31 and is WARL.
RULE 6: When SXLEN=32, the ASID field of satp is at bits 30-22 and is WARL.
RULE 7: When SXLEN=32, the PPN field of satp is at bits 21-0 and is WARL.
RULE 8: When SXLEN=64, the MODE field of satp is at bits 63-60 and is WARL.
RULE 9: When SXLEN=64, the ASID field of satp is at bits 59-44 and is WARL.
RULE 10: When SXLEN=64, the PPN field of satp is at bits 43-0 and is WARL.
RULE 11: Storing a PPN in satp, rather than a physical address, supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB for RV32.
RULE 12: The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers.
RULE 13: When satp.MODE=Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses, and there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme.
RULE 14: To select MODE=Bare, software must write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 30-0 when SXLEN=32, or bits 59-0 when SXLEN=64).
RULE 15: Attempting to select MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining fields of satp has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
RULE 16: When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3 are designated for custom use.
RULE 17: When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7] not equal to 3 are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 18: When SXLEN=64, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 19: When SXLEN=32, the only other valid setting for satp.MODE besides Bare is Sv32.
RULE 20: When SXLEN=64, three paged virtual-memory schemes are defined for satp: Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
RULE 21: When SXLEN=64, the remaining satp.MODE settings are reserved for future use and may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.
RULE 22: If satp is written with an unsupported MODE, the entire write has no effect and no fields in satp are modified.
RULE 23: The number of implemented ASID bits (ASIDLEN) may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the ASID field of satp, then reading back the value in satp to see which bit positions in the ASID field hold a one.
RULE 24: If ASIDLEN > 0, ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] in satp is writable.
RULE 25: The maximal value of ASIDLEN (ASIDMAX) for satp is 9 for Sv32 or 16 for Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
RULE 26: The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.

RULE 28: Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.
RULE 29: Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations.
RULE 30: Writing satp does not imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.
RULE 31: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE=0 indicates Bare, which is no translation or protection.
RULE 32: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE=1 indicates Sv32, which is page-based 32-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 33: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE=0 indicates Bare, which is no translation or protection.
RULE 34: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 1-7 are reserved for standard use.
RULE 35: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE=8 indicates Sv39, which is page-based 39-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 36: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE=9 indicates Sv48, which is page-based 48-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 37: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE=10 indicates Sv57, which is page-based 57-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 38: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE=11 is reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 39: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 12-13 are reserved for standard use.
RULE 40: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 14-15 are designated for custom use.

The following description:

* Implementations must only perform implicit reads of the translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry, and must only raise exceptions for implicit accesses that are generated as a result of instruction execution, not those that are performed speculatively.

* Changing satp.MODE from Bare to other modes and vice versa takes effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.

* Changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.

* A property applies where an implementation may use any translation for an address that was valid at any time since the most recent SFENCE.VMA that subsumes that address, even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

* When software recycles an ASID (i.e., reassociates it with a different page table), it should first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID, then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID. Alternatively, software can execute the same SFENCE.VMA instruction while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID, it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.

* If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, or software chooses to always use ASID 0, then after every satp write, software should execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0.

* For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero (always Bare), attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: Implementations must only perform implicit reads of the translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry.
RULE 2: Implementations must only raise exceptions for implicit accesses generated as a result of instruction execution, and not for those performed speculatively, when accessing translation data structures pointed to by the satp register or subsequent valid entries.
RULE 3: Changing satp.MODE from Bare to other modes and vice versa takes effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.
RULE 4: Changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.
RULE 5: An implementation may use any translation for an address that was valid at any time since the most recent SFENCE.VMA that subsumes that address, even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.
RULE 6: When software recycles an ASID, it should first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID, then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID.
RULE 7: When software recycles an ASID, software can execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1= x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID, it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.
RULE 8: If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, or software chooses to always use ASID 0, then after every satp write, software should execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0.
RULE 9: For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero (always Bare), attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.
======================================================= END - 55 =======================================================

The following description:

* When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register, supervisor virtual addresses are translated into supervisor physical addresses via a two-level page table.

RULE 3: When the value of satp.MODE is Bare, the 32-bit virtual address is translated unmodified into a 32-bit physical address, and then that physical address is zero-extended into a 34-bit machine-level physical address, given an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address.
RULE 4: The physical page number of the root page table is stored in the satp register.

The following description:

* The vsatp register is a VSXLEN-bit read/write register that is VS-mode's version of supervisor register satp.
* When V=1, vsatp substitutes for the usual satp, so instructions that normally read or modify satp actually access vsatp instead.
* vsatp controls VS-stage address translation, the first stage of two-stage translation for guest virtual addresses.
* The vsatp register is considered active for the purposes of the address-translation algorithm unless the effective privilege mode is U and hstatus.HU=0.
* When V=0, a write to vsatp with an unsupported MODE value is either ignored as it is for satp, or the fields of vsatp are treated as WARL in the normal way.
* When V=1, a write to satp with an unsupported MODE value is ignored and no write to vsatp is effected.
* When V=0, vsatp does not directly affect the behavior of the machine, unless a virtual-machine load/store (HLV, HLVX, or HSV) or the MPRV feature in the mstatus register is used to execute a load or store as though V=1.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The vsatp register is a VSXLEN-bit read/write register that serves as the VS-mode version of the supervisor register satp.
RULE 2: When V=1, instructions that normally read or modify satp actually access vsatp instead.
RULE 3: The vsatp register controls VS-stage address translation, which is the first stage of two-stage translation for guest virtual addresses.
RULE 4: The vsatp register is considered active for the purposes of the address-translation algorithm unless the effective privilege mode is U and hstatus.HU=0.
RULE 5: When V=0, a write to vsatp with an unsupported MODE value is either ignored as it is for satp, or the fields of vsatp are treated as WARL in the normal way.
RULE 6: When V=1, a write to satp with an unsupported MODE value is ignored and no write to vsatp is effected.
RULE 7: When V=0, vsatp does not directly affect the behavior of the machine, unless a virtual-machine load/store (HLV, HLVX, or HSV) or the MPRV feature in the mstatus register is used to execute a load or store as though V=1.
======================================================= END - 63 =======================================================

* No mechanism is provided to atomically change vsatp and hgatp together.

The following description:

The scounteren CSR is a 32-bit register that controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.

* When the CY bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the cycle register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
* When the CY bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the cycle register is permitted.
* When the TM bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the time register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
* When the TM bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the time register is permitted.
* When the IR bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the instret register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
* When the IR bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the instret register is permitted.
* When the HPM n bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
* When the HPM n bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the hpmcountern register is permitted.

scounteren must be implemented. Any of the bits may be read-only zero, indicating reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode. They are effectively WARL fields.

The setting of a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable. U-mode may only access a counter if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The scounteren CSR is a 32-bit register that controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.
RULE 2: When the CY bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the cycle register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: When the CY bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the cycle register is permitted.
RULE 4: When the TM bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the time register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 5: When the TM bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the time register is permitted.
RULE 6: When the IR bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the instret register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 7: When the IR bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the instret register is permitted.
RULE 8: When the HPM n bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 9: When the HPM n bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the hpmcountern register is permitted.
RULE 10: scounteren must be implemented.
RULE 11: Any of the bits in scounteren may be read-only zero, indicating reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode.
RULE 12: The bits in scounteren are effectively WARL fields.
RULE 13: The setting of a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable.
RULE 14: U-mode may only access a counter if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.

The following description:

* When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception (code 22) is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if the attempted instruction is HS-qualified but is prevented from executing when V=1 either due to insufficient privilege or because the instruction is expressly disabled by a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren.

============================================================
RULE 1: When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception (code 22) is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if an attempted HS-qualified instruction is prevented from executing due to being expressly disabled by a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren.
RULE 2: In VU-mode, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access a non-high-half counter CSR when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.
RULE 3: In VU-mode, if XLEN=32, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access a high-half counter CSR when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.
======================================================= END - 74 =======================================================
